The computation is performed entirely on\n// the GPU; the host only initializes/launches and copies the final grid back\n// for result reporting and validation.\n\n#include <algorithm>\n#include <chrono>\n#include <cmath>\n#include <cstdio>\n#include <cstdlib>\n#include <cstring>\n#include <vector>\n\n#include <cuda_runtime.h>\n\n#include \"../common/results_output.hpp\"\n\nusing Real = double;\n\n#define CUDA_CHECK(call)                                                                 \\\n    do {                                                                                 \\\n        cudaError_t err__ = (call);                                                      \\\n        if (err__ != cudaSuccess) {                                                      \\\n            fprintf(stderr, \"CUDA error %s at %s:%d: %s\\n\", #call, __FILE__, __LINE__,   \\\n                    cudaGetErrorString(err__));                                          \\\n            exit(1);                                                                     \\\n        }                                                                                \\\n    } while (0)\n\n// 3D index calculation\n__host__ __device__ inline constexpr size_t idx3(const size_t x, const size_t y, const size_t z,\n                                                 const size_t nx, const size_t ny) noexcept {\n    return z * (nx * ny) + y * nx + x;\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Grid initialization kernel: grid[idx] = (idx % 19) * 1.0\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n__global__ void initializeGridKernel(Real* __restrict__ grid, const size_t n) {\n    const size_t stride = (size_t)gridDim.x * blockDim.x;\n    for (size_t idx = (size_t)blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x; idx < n; idx += stride) {\n        grid[idx] = (Real)(idx % 19) * 1.0;\n    }\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// 7-point stencil kernel.\n//\n// Thread-block tiling: each block covers a TX x TY tile of interior points in\n// the x-y plane and marches along z.  The x/y neighbours are served from a\n// shared-memory tile (with a one-cell halo); the z-1 / z / z+1 planes are kept\n// in registers and rotated as the block walks along z, so each input element\n// is loaded from global memory essentially once per iteration.\n//\n// Floating point semantics match the CPU version exactly: the same summation\n// order and a true IEEE division by 7.0 (no fast-math, no FMA contraction is\n// possible since there are no multiplies).\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\nconstexpr int TX = 32;   // tile width  (x)\nconstexpr int TY = 8;    // tile height (y)\nconstexpr int ZCHUNK = 64;  // number of z-planes processed by one block\n\n__global__ void __launch_bounds__(TX * TY)\nstencilKernel(const Real* __restrict__ input, Real* __restrict__ output,\n              const int nx, const int ny, const int nz) {\n    __shared__ Real tile[TY + 2][TX + 2];\n\n    const int tx = threadIdx.x;\n    const int ty = threadIdx.y;\n\n    // Global coordinates of this thread's interior point (offset by 1 for the boundary).\n    const int x = blockIdx.x * TX + tx + 1;\n    const int y = blockIdx.y * TY + ty + 1;\n\n    const int z0 = blockIdx.z * ZCHUNK + 1;               // first interior z of this block\n    const int z1 = min(z0 + ZCHUNK, nz - 1);              // one past the last interior z\n\n    const bool active = (x < nx - 1) && (y < ny - 1);\n\n    const size_t plane = (size_t)nx * ny;\n\n    // Clamp coordinates for inactive threads so that their (unused) loads stay in bounds.\n    const int xc = active ? x : 1;\n    const int yc = active ? y : 1;\n\n    const Real* in_col = input + (size_t)yc * nx + xc;\n\n    Real below  = in_col[(size_t)(z0 - 1) * plane];\n    Real center = in_col[(size_t)z0 * plane];\n\n    for (int z = z0; z < z1; ++z) {\n        const Real above = in_col[(size_t)(z + 1) * plane];\n\n        // Load the current plane into shared memory (interior part of the tile).\n        const Real* in_plane = input + (size_t)z * plane;\n        tile[ty + 1][tx + 1] = center;\n\n        // Halo in x\n        if (tx == 0) {\n            tile[ty + 1][0] = in_plane[(size_t)yc * nx + (xc - 1)];\n        }\n        if (tx == TX - 1 || xc == nx - 2) {\n            tile[ty + 1][tx + 2] = in_plane[(size_t)yc * nx + (xc + 1)];\n        }\n        // Halo in y\n        if (ty == 0) {\n            tile[0][tx + 1] = in_plane[(size_t)(yc - 1) * nx + xc];\n        }\n        if (ty == TY - 1 || yc == ny - 2) {\n            tile[ty + 2][tx + 1] = in_plane[(size_t)(yc + 1) * nx + xc];\n        }\n\n        __syncthreads();\n\n        if (active) {\n            const Real left   = tile[ty + 1][tx];\n            const Real right  = tile[ty + 1][tx + 2];\n            const Real front  = tile[ty][tx + 1];\n            const Real back   = tile[ty + 2][tx + 1];\n            const Real bottom = below;\n            const Real top    = above;\n\n            // Simple averaging stencil (identical evaluation order to the CPU code)\n            output[(size_t)z * plane + (size_t)y * nx + x] =\n                (center + left + right + front + back + bottom + top) / 7.0;\n        }\n\n        __syncthreads();\n\n        below  = center;\n        center = above;\n    }\n}\n\n// Launch one stencil iteration on the device.\n//\n// Boundary handling: the CPU version copies input boundary values to the output\n// every iteration.  Since the boundary of both buffers is initialised to the\n// same values before the first iteration (see main), and only interior points\n// are ever written, the boundary copy is a no-op and is skipped here.  The\n// resulting grids are bit-for-bit identical to the CPU version.\nvoid stencilIteration(const Real* input, Real* output,\n                      const size_t nx, const size_t ny, const size_t nz) {\n    if (nx < 3 || ny < 3 || nz < 3) return;  // no interior points\n\n    const int inx = (int)(nx - 2);\n    const int iny = (int)(ny - 2);\n    const int inz = (int)(nz - 2);\n\n    dim3 block(TX, TY, 1);\n    dim3 grid((inx + TX - 1) / TX, (iny + TY - 1) / TY, (inz + ZCHUNK - 1) / ZCHUNK);\n\n    stencilKernel<<<grid, block>>>(input, output, (int)nx, (int)ny, (int)nz);\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaGetLastError());\n}\n\nbool validateResult(const std::vector<Real>& grid, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nx, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t ny, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nz) {\n    // Simple sanity checks\n    \n    // 1. No NaN or Inf values\n    for (const auto& val : grid) {\n        if (std::isnan(val) || std::isinf(val)) {\n            printf(\"Validation failed: found NaN or Inf value\\n\");\n            return false;\n        }\n    }\n    \n    // 2. Values should be reasonable (bounded)\n    Real minVal = grid[0];\n    Real maxVal = grid[0];\n    for (const auto& val : grid) {\n        minVal = std::min(minVal, val);\n        maxVal = std::max(maxVal, val);\n    }\n    \n    printf(\"Value range: [%.6f, %.6f]\\n\", minVal, maxVal);\n    \n    // After averaging, values should be somewhat bounded\n    if (maxVal > 1e6 || minVal < -1e6) {\n        printf(\"Validation failed: values out of expected range\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n    \n    // 3. Boundary values should not change significantly\n    // (they are copied, so they should be close to initial values)\n    \n    return true;\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) {\n    printf(\"Usage: %s [options]\\n\", progName);\n    printf(\"Options:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -x <num>     Grid size in X dimension (default: 128)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -y <num>     Grid size in Y dimension (default: same as X)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -z <num>     Grid size in Z dimension (default: same as X)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -i <num>     Number of iterations (default: 10)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -v           Enable validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -r           Print results for external validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -h           Show this help message\\n\");\n}\n\nint main(int argc, char** argv) {\n    size_t nx = 128;\n    size_t ny = 0;  // Will be set to nx if not specified\n    size_t nz = 0;  // Will be set to nx if not specified\n    int iterations = 10;\n    bool validate = false;\n    bool printResults = false;\n    \n    // Parse command line arguments\n    for (int i = 1; i < argc; ++i) {\n        if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-x\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n            nx = atoi(argv[++i]);\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-y\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n            ny = atoi(argv[++i]);\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-z\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n            nz = atoi(argv[++i]);\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-i\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n            iterations = atoi(argv[++i]);\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-v\") == 0) {\n            validate = true;\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-r\") == 0) {\n            printResults = true;\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-h\") == 0) {\n            printUsage(argv[0]);\n            return 0;\n        } else {\n            printf(\"Unknown option: %s\\n\", argv[i]);\n            printUsage(argv[0]);\n            return 1;\n        }\n    }\n    \n    if (ny == 0) ny = nx;\n    if (nz == 0) nz = nx;\n    \n    printf(\"3D Stencil Benchmark\\n\");\n    printf(\"Grid size: %zu x %zu x %zu\\n\", nx, ny, nz);\n    printf(\"Iterations: %d\\n\", iterations);\n    printf(\"Validation: %s\\n\", validate ? \"enabled\" : \"disabled\");\n    \n    size_t gridSize = nx * ny * nz;\n    \n    // Allocate grids on the device (double buffering)\n    Real* d_grid1 = nullptr;\n    Real* d_grid2 = nullptr;\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaMalloc(&d_grid1, gridSize * sizeof(Real)));\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaMalloc(&d_grid2, gridSize * sizeof(Real)));\n    \n    // Initialize\n    printf(\"Initializing grid...\\n\");\n    {\n        const int threads = 256;\n        size_t blocks = (gridSize + threads - 1) / threads;\n        blocks = std::min<size_t>(std::max<size_t>(blocks, 1), 65535 * 16);\n        initializeGridKernel<<<(unsigned)blocks, threads>>>(d_grid1, gridSize);\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaGetLastError());\n    }\n    // Both buffers carry identical boundary values; the stencil only writes interior\n    // points, which makes the per-iteration boundary copy of the CPU version redundant.\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaMemcpy(d_grid2, d_grid1, gridSize * sizeof(Real), cudaMemcpyDeviceToDevice));\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaDeviceSynchronize());\n    \n    // Run stencil iterations\n    printf(\"Running stencil computation...\\n\");\n    auto start = std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now();\n    \n    for (int iter = 0; iter < iterations; ++iter) {\n        if (iter % 2 == 0) {\n            stencilIteration(d_grid1, d_grid2, nx, ny, nz);\n        } else {\n            stencilIteration(d_grid2, d_grid1, nx, ny, nz);\n        }\n    }\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaDeviceSynchronize());\n    \n    auto end = std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now();\n    auto duration = std::chrono::duration_cast<std::chrono::milliseconds>(end - start);\n    \n    printf(\"Computation time: %ld ms\\n\", duration.count());\n    \n    // Calculate performance metrics\n    double cellUpdates = (double)((nx-2) * (ny-2) * (nz-2)) * iterations;\n    double mcups = cellUpdates / (duration.count() / 1000.0) / 1e6;  // Million cell updates per second\n    printf(\"Performance: %.3f MCellUpdates/s\\n\", mcups);\n    \n    // Copy the final grid back to the host for result reporting / validation\n    std::vector<Real> finalGrid(gridSize);\n    const Real* d_final = (iterations % 2 == 0) ? d_grid1 : d_grid2;\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaMemcpy(finalGrid.data(), d_final, gridSize * sizeof(Real), cudaMemcpyDeviceToHost));\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaFree(d_grid1));\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaFree(d_grid2));\n    \n    // Print results for external validation\n    if (printResults) {\n        print_results(finalGrid, \"Grid\");\n    }\n    \n    // Validation\n    if (validate) {\n        printf(\"Validating result...\\n\");\n        bool valid = validateResult(finalGrid, nx, ny, nz);\n        \n        if (valid) {\n            printf(\"Validation: PASSED\\n\");\n            return 0;\n        } else {\n            printf(\"Validation: FAILED\\n\");\n            return 1;\n        }\n    }\n    \n    return 0;\n}\nEOF\ncat > Zero, subnormals, Inf, NaN and extreme\n// magnitudes take the hardware division, so the result is bit-identical to\n// x / 7.0 for every input.  Verified against x / 7.0 on 5e10 inputs covering\n// arbitrary bit patterns, specials, and the value ranges of this benchmark.\n// The guard uses only integer ops on the exponent field so the FP64 pipe sees\n// exactly 3 instructions (DMUL + 2 DFMA) instead of ~15.\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n__device__ __forceinline__ Real div7(const Real x) {\n    const Real y = 1.0 / 7.0;  // compile-time RN(1/7)\n    const Real q = x * y;\n    const Real r = fma(-q, 7.0, x);\n    Real result = fma(r, y, q);\n    // Biased exponent in [63, 1983]  <=>  |x| in [2^-960, 2^960]\n    const unsigned e = ((unsigned)__double2hiint(x) >> 20) & 0x7ffu;\n    if (e - 63u > 1920u) result = x / 7.0;\n    return result;\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// 7-point stencil kernel: one thread per interior cell.\n//\n// Thread blocks are BX x BY x BZ tiles of interior points; the x dimension maps to\n// consecutive addresses so each warp reads/writes fully coalesced 8-byte elements,\n// and the x/y/z neighbours are served from L1/L2 (every input element is touched\n// by 7 threads, but only once from DRAM).  The stencil is FP64-throughput bound on\n// GeForce parts, so the one-cell-per-thread form with maximal occupancy and no\n// synchronisation won over shared-memory / register-marching variants.\n//\n// Floating point semantics match the CPU version exactly: the same left-to-right\n// summation order and a correctly rounded division by 7.0 (see div7).  No\n// fast-math flags are used and there are no multiplies that could be contracted.\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\nconstexpr int BX = 64;  // block extent in x (consecutive addresses)\nconstexpr int BY = 2;   // block extent in y\nconstexpr int BZ = 2;   // block extent in z\n\n__global__ void __launch_bounds__(BX * BY * BZ)\nstencilKernel(const Real* __restrict__ input, Real* __restrict__ output,\n              const int nx, const int ny, const int nz) {\n    // Interior coordinates (offset by 1 for the boundary layer).\n    const int x = blockIdx.x * BX + threadIdx.x + 1;\n    const int y = blockIdx.y * BY + threadIdx.y + 1;\n    const int z = blockIdx.z * BZ + threadIdx.z + 1;\n    if (x >= nx - 1 || y >= ny - 1 || z >= nz - 1) return;\n\n    const size_t plane = (size_t)nx * ny;\n    const size_t idx = (size_t)z * plane + (size_t)y * nx + x;\n\n    const Real center = __ldg(input + idx);\n    const Real left   = __ldg(input + idx - 1);\n    const Real right  = __ldg(input + idx + 1);\n    const Real front  = __ldg(input + idx - nx);\n    const Real back   = __ldg(input + idx + nx);\n    const Real bottom = __ldg(input + idx - plane);\n    const Real top    = __ldg(input + idx + plane);\n\n    // Simple averaging stencil (identical evaluation order to the CPU code)\n    output[idx] = div7(center + left + right + front + back + bottom + top);\n}\n\n// Launch one stencil iteration on the device (asynchronous).\n//\n// Boundary handling: the CPU version copies input boundary values to the output\n// every iteration.  Since the boundary of both buffers is initialised to the\n// same values before the first iteration (see main), and only interior points\n// are ever written, the boundary copy is a no-op and is skipped here.  The\n// resulting grids are bit-for-bit identical to the CPU version.\nvoid stencilIteration(const Real* input, Real* output,\n                      const size_t nx, const size_t ny, const size_t nz) {\n    if (nx < 3 || ny < 3 || nz < 3) return;  // no interior points\n\n    const int inx = (int)(nx - 2);\n    const int iny = (int)(ny - 2);\n    const int inz = (int)(nz - 2);\n\n    const dim3 block(BX, BY, BZ);\n    const dim3 grid((inx + BX - 1) / BX, (iny + BY - 1) / BY, (inz + BZ - 1) / BZ);\n\n    stencilKernel<<<grid, block>>>(input, output, (int)nx, (int)ny, (int)nz);\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaGetLastError());\n}\n\n'''\ns=s[:start]+new+s[end:]\nopen('stencil3d.cpp','w').write(s)\nEOF\ngrep -n \"idx3\" stencil3d.cpp | head; cd /tmp/cuda_build && make 2>&1 | grep -E \"warning|error|Built\"","description":"Install final kernel into repo source and rebuild"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":32,"cache_creation_input_tokens":3217,"cache_read_input_tokens":58942,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":3217},"output_tokens":7,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"global","speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"ea0703c6-9781-40ec-a34a-9aa306834dac","uuid":"7d2864ce-42c5-4391-a6b9-b8b7e6a33e78","timestamp":"2026-10-30T23:20:34.567Z","request_id":"req_011CgZH7ABA3fsC5p51hMf2q"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-fable-5-1","id":"msg_011CgZHJvkMBzxfgbDP1Co4T","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The stencil3d benchmark now runs entirely on the GPU with CUDA, and its output is bit-identical to the original CPU program.\n\n**What changed** (only the two existing files; executable name is still `stencil3d`):\n\n- `stencil3d/stencil3d.cpp` is now CUDA source. Grid initialization, the stencil iterations, and double buffering all happen in device memory. The final grid is copied back once for the `-r` results and `-v` validation. The command-line interface and all printed output are unchanged.\n- `stencil3d/CMakeLists.txt` enables the CUDA language, compiles the `.cpp` file as CUDA via a source-file property, and builds for sm_70 through sm_90 by default. No new dependencies beyond the CUDA toolkit already on the machine.\n\n**Kernel design.** One thread per interior cell in 64x2x2 blocks with coalesced loads and no synchronization. I also tried shared-memory tiling and register z-marching variants. They were not faster, because the kernel is bound by FP64 throughput, not memory bandwidth. The RTX 3090 runs double precision at 1/64 rate, and the correctly rounded division by 7.0 alone expands to about 15 dependent instructions.\n\n**The division trick.** To keep exact semantics without that cost, the kernel uses Markstein's reciprocal correction: multiply by the rounded reciprocal of 7, compute the exact residual with one FMA, and correct with a second FMA. This gives the correctly rounded quotient for normal inputs. Zero, subnormal, infinite, NaN, and extreme-magnitude inputs fall back to true hardware division via an integer check on the exponent field. I verified it against `x / 7.0` on 51 billion inputs, including arbitrary bit patterns and specials, with zero mismatches.\n\n**Correctness.** Output with `-r` (sum, min, max, samples, hash) is byte-for-byte identical to the original CPU build for 128³, 256³, 64x96x80, and seven other odd shapes including 3³, 4³, and zero iterations. The per-iteration boundary copy from the original is replaced by a one-time device copy before the loop, which produces the same grids since boundaries never change.\n\n**Performance** (million cell updates per second, CPU original vs. CUDA):\n\n| Grid, iterations | CPU | CUDA |\n|---|---|---|\n| 128³, 10 (default) | 465 | ~21,000 |\n| 256³, 100 | 413 | ~23,000 |\n| 512³, 50 | n/a | ~24,800 |\n\nOne small deviation: the throughput line is now computed from the sub-millisecond timer because GPU runs of the default size finish in under 1 ms, which would otherwise print \"inf\".
